### [51] [ID-VTG: Image-Disambiguated Video Temporal Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20127)
*Minghang Zheng,Jingli Wei,Hongyi Yang,Yang Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes Image-Disambiguated Video Temporal Grounding (ID-VTG) that uses a reference image plus text to precisely localize instances of described actions, with a dual-branch fast-slow architecture and two learnable tokens for hard negatives and text-irrelevant events. Introduces two benchmarks (IDVTG-Gym, IDVTG-InternVid) and achieves state-of-the-art results; code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Disambiguation in video temporal grounding is hard when visually similar entities are involved; relying on a single text description can be insufficient. A multimodal query (image + text) can anchor the target instance and improve precision.

Method: A dual-branch fast-slow architecture (fast: proposal generation; slow: frame-level alignment to the reference image). Introduces two learnable tokens: 1) Compare Token to probe for the presence of the target instance (hard negatives), and 2) Depress Value to represent text-irrelevant events. Training pushes proposals that the Compare Token flags as not containing the target toward the Depress Value, improving disambiguation.

Result: Empirical evaluation shows state-of-the-art performance on the proposed benchmarks (IDVTG-Gym and IDVTG-InternVid). The work provides code for reproduction.

Conclusion: Image-guided disambiguation via the VGD-Agg framework effectively mitigates ambiguity in VTG tasks, validates the usefulness of a reference image for disambiguation, and offers datasets and code to foster further research.

Abstract: Video Temporal Grounding (VTG) faces significant challenges when natural language queries must distinguish between multiple events involving visually similar entities, particularly when relying on fine-grained visual attributes that are difficult to describe accurately in words alone. To address this, we introduce Image-Disambiguated Video Temporal Grounding (ID-VTG), a task that leverages multimodal queries combining a reference image and a text description to precisely localize segments where a specific instance performs a described action. To facilitate research, we construct two benchmarks: IDVTG-Gym, focusing on fine-grained, compositionally ordered gymnastics actions with athletes in similar uniforms; and IDVTG-InternVid, an open-world dataset featuring diverse entities (e.g., humans, animals, fictional characters) and significant temporal distractors. Methodologically, we propose the Visually-Guided Disambiguation Aggregation (VGD-Agg) framework based on a dual-branch fast-slow architecture. The fast branch efficiently generates preliminary event proposals, while the slow branch performs fine-grained frame-level matching between video frames and the reference image. We enhance discriminability via two learnable tokens: a Compare Token, which represents hard negatives to probe for the presence of the target instance (as referred to by the query image), and a Depress Value, which represents text-irrelevant events. Proposals that the Compare Token identifies as lacking the target instance are pushed toward the Depress Value, thus easing disambiguation via the text query. Extensive experiments validate our approach, which achieves state-of-the-art results on the proposed benchmarks. Code is available at https://github.com/oceanflowlab/ID-VTG.

</details>


### [52] [Feature Evolution and Migration during Vision Transformer Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20134)
*Joonas Järve,Halil Ibrahim Aysel,Tarun Khajuria,Meelis Kull*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Visualizes feature evolution in Vision Transformers by tracking sparse autoencoder features across layer-time (epoch-layer grid), revealing early feature migration and depth-based stability patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand feature-level dynamics in ViTs that are not captured by representation-level similarity, enabling insight into how features migrate and stabilize during training.

Method: Train ViTs; use CLS-token representations; apply Sparse Autoencoders to extract sparse features; compare activation profiles across epoch–layer pairs; analyze feature migration and stability.

Result: Migration is concentrated early in training; feature migration occurs more toward earlier layers; deeper layers stabilize earlier and more strongly than shallow ones; migration declines as feature organization stabilizes; the framework serves as a tool to understand ViT learning.

Conclusion: A framework for analyzing feature evolution in ViTs that reveals migration and stabilization patterns, providing a clearer view of how ViTs learn beyond representation similarity.

Abstract: We present a novel view on feature evolution in Vision Transformers (ViTs) by visualizing the training process over two dimensions -- network depth (layer) and training time (epochs). We employ Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to extract candidate sparse features from CLS-token representations and compare their activation profiles across epoch--layer pairs. This allows us to study feature-level dynamics that are not directly visible from representation-level similarity measures. Furthermore, we demonstrate how this framework of feature evolution allows us to describe feature migration, the change in the layer where a feature is most detectable during training. Our experiments show that migration is concentrated early in training, occurs more often toward earlier layers than toward deeper layers, and declines as feature organization stabilizes. We further find that deeper layers stabilize earlier and more strongly than shallow layers. The results show that our approach can be employed as a tool for understanding how ViTs learn and evolve.

</details>


### [53] [DPC-Net: Dual-Prior Collaborative Network for All-in-One Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20141)
*Zhaokun He,Kangbiao Shi,Axi Niu,Jian Jin,Peng Wu,Wei Dong,Qingsen Yan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DPC-Net for all-in-one image restoration leverages degradation-semantic priors and low-level visual priors via DAN, DSMM, DPCR, guided by a Vision-Language Model, to preserve structure and semantics and outperform SOTA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: AiOIR methods typically model degradations without semantic context and lack strong low-level priors, leading to distortions; integrating degradation semantics with visual priors aims to improve fidelity.

Method: Degradation-Aware Network (DAN) extracts degradation-semantic coupled features; Vision-Language Model supervises DAN by matching feature distributions; Degradation-Semantic Modulation Module (DSMM) injects guidance into coupled representations; decoding uses knowledge bases for low-level priors; Dual-Prior Collaborative Reconstruction Module (DPCR) fuses priors for restoration.

Result: Empirical evaluation on multiple restoration benchmarks shows that DPC-Net achieves superior performance compared with state-of-the-art AiOIR methods.

Conclusion: Jointly leveraging degradation-semantic and low-level priors via a dual-prior framework yields higher-fidelity restorations with preserved structure and semantics.

Abstract: All-in-One Image Restoration (AiOIR) aims to handle diverse degradations within a unified model. However, existing methods often overlook image semantics in degradation modeling and lack low-level visual priors during reconstruction, leading to structural distortions and semantic inconsistencies. To address these issues, we propose a novel Dual-Prior Collaborative Network (DPC-Net), which achieves high-quality restoration by jointly exploiting degradation-semantic coupled priors and low-level visual priors. Specifically, degraded images are fed into a Degradation-Aware Network (DAN) to extract degradation-semantic coupled features. To this end, a Vision-Language Model (VLM) supervises DAN by constraining its features distribution, introducing image semantics into the encoding of degradation patterns. A Degradation-Semantic Modulation Module (DSMM) further translates this guidance into degradation-semantic coupling and propagates coupled representations to the decoder. During decoding, knowledge bases provide low-level visual priors, and the Dual-Prior Collaborative Reconstruction Module (DPCR) integrates dual-prior information to guide degradation removal while preserving structure and semantics, producing high-fidelity restored images. Extensive experiments on multiple restoration benchmarks demonstrate that DPC-Net achieves superior performance against state-of-the-art AiOIR methods.

</details>


### [54] [PelviNeXt: A Modality-Agnostic Hybrid Network for Pelvic Imaging in Women's Health](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20144)
*Siam Tahsin Bhuiyan,Rashedur Rahman,Sefatul Wasi,Halima Khatun,Ashraful Islam,AKM Mahbubur Rahman,Saadia Binte Alam,M Ashraful Amin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PelviNeXt is a modality-agnostic pelvic-imaging architecture (dense conv features + H-CBAM + MSFM + TH-MHSA) that achieves state-of-the-art results on pelvis ultrasound (PCOSGen) after deduplication and integrity auditing, and on pelvic X-ray (PXR150). It claims cross-modality effectiveness without task-specific changes, and highlights the importance of data curation in under-resourced women's health imaging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Under-resourced women's health imaging with scarce, public, well-annotated benchmarks for PCOS ultrasound and pelvic fractures; prevalence of data contamination and lack of integrity-aware evaluation protocols; need for a unified, data-efficient architecture that can operate across modalities.

Method: A single architecture (PelviNeXt) combining a dense convolutional feature extractor, hierarchical channel-spatial attention (H-CBAM), a multi-scale fusion module (MSFM), and talking-heads multi-head self-attention (TH-MHSA), applied without modification to ultrasound and X-ray inputs. The authors audit and deduplicate the PCOSGen ultrasound dataset using perceptual hashing, release a cleaned version, and evaluate via 5-fold cross-validation. They benchmark on PXR150 X-ray data, reporting comprehensive metrics. Ablation studies show each component contributes to performance on both tasks.

Result: On PXR150, PelviNeXt achieves state-of-the-art performance across accuracy, recall, specificity, and AUROC. For PCOSGen, the work exposes extensive exact/near-duplicate contamination and provides a deduplicated dataset along with an integrity-audited evaluation protocol and baseline under 5-fold CV. Ablations indicate all architectural components are beneficial for both ultrasound and X-ray tasks.

Conclusion: A unified pelvic-imaging architecture can serve as a robust foundation across modalities in data-scarce, under-researched areas of women's health. This work emphasizes the value of data integrity and standardized evaluation, while revealing potential for broader application and future external validation.

Abstract: Women's health remains substantially under-resourced in medical imaging research, with pelvic pathologies such as polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS) and pelvic fracture both suffering from a scarcity of public, well-annotated benchmark data despite their clinical importance. We introduce PelviNeXt, a modality-agnostic hybrid architecture combining a dense convolutional feature extractor, hierarchical channel-spatial attention (H-CBAM), a multi-scale fusion module (MSFM), and talking-heads multi-head self-attention (TH-MHSA), applied without modification to both pelvic ultrasound and X-ray inputs. While benchmarking PelviNeXt on PCOSGen, the only gynaecologist-annotated public PCOS ultrasound dataset, we identified extensive exact and near-duplicate contamination within and across the dataset. We audit this contamination via perceptual hashing, publicly release a deduplicated version of the dataset, and establish the first integrity-audited evaluation protocol and baseline for PCOSGen under 5-fold cross-validation. On the only publicly available pelvic fracture X-ray dataset (PXR150), PelviNeXt exceeds previously reported state-of-the-art results across accuracy, recall, specificity, and AUROC. Ablation studies confirm that each architectural component contributes to performance on both tasks. Our results demonstrate that a single architecture, applied without task-specific modification, can serve as a reliable foundation for pelvic imaging across modalities in data-scarce, under-researched areas of women's health.

</details>


### [55] [Artificial Intelligence for Workflow Analysis in Colorectal Surgery: A Multicentric, Cross-Procedural Development and Generalization Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20154)
*Pietro Mascagni,Julia Alekseenko,Pooja P Jain,Marta Goglia,Andrea Balla,Ludovica Baldari,Gianfranco Silecchia,Claudio Fiorillo,Vincenzo Tondolo,Salvador Morales-Conde,Luigi Boni,Sergio Alfieri,Nicolas Padoy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A multicenter deep learning model (AI-ColoWorkflow) for automated MIS-CRS phase and step recognition using DINOv3 features and a hierarchical temporal CNN; pooling multicenter data improves generalization, outperforming center- and procedure-specific models for phases but step recognition remains challenging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MIS-CRS outcomes show substantial variability and current manual video-based analysis is labor-intensive; automated, scalable, cross-center workflow analysis is needed to standardize phase/step recognition and support performance assessment.

Method: Fine-tuned DINOv3 vision transformer extracts per-frame features; hierarchical multi-stage temporal convolutional network performs joint phase and step recognition; trained on pooled multicenter and public MIS-CRS videos with ColoWorkflow annotations; evaluated against center-specific and procedure-specific models on held-out data using macro F1, balanced accuracy, precision, and recall.

Result: Phase recognition: macro F1 73.01%±10.27; balanced accuracy 73.43%. Step recognition: macro F1 39.82%±7.06; balanced accuracy 38.65%. Global model generally outperforms center- and procedure-specific models for phase recognition; procedure-specific step models retain advantages for some procedures. Generalization (phase) mean F1 48.42%.

Conclusion: A single pooled AI-ColoWorkflow model can reliably recognize MIS-CRS phases and generalizes comparably or better than center- or procedure-specific models for phase recognition; procedure-specific steps may still benefit from targeted models, suggesting hybrid training strategies.

Abstract: Minimally invasive colorectal surgeries (MIS-CRS) are characterised by significant variability and inconsistent outcomes. ColoWorkflow, a tool for the video-based assessment (VBA) of MIS-CRS workflow, was recently validated. However, manual VBA is time-consuming, limiting implementation. This study presents AI-ColoWorkflow, a deep learning model for automated surgical workflow analysis across MIS-CRS. Operative videos of MIS-CRS were collected from 4 centres and a publicly available dataset. Phases and steps were manually annotated according to ColoWorkflow. A deep learning model combining a fine-tuned DINOv3 vision transformer for per-frame visual feature extraction with a hierarchical multi-stage temporal convolutional network was jointly optimized for phase and step recognition. The model trained on pooled multicentric data, namely AI-ColoWorkflow was compared against centre-specific and procedure-specific models on a held-out test set. The following metrics were used for evaluation: macro F1 score, balanced accuracy, precision, and recall. AI-ColoWorkflow achieved a macro F1 of 73.01% $\pm$ 10.27 (balanced accuracy 73.43%) for phase recognition and 39.82% $\pm$ 7.06 (balanced accuracy 38.65%) for step recognition. The global model outperformed centre- and procedure-specific models in most experiments except procedure-specific step recognition. In the generalization analysis, mean F1 was 48.42% for phase recognition. AI-ColoWorkflow can reliably recognize MIS-CRS phases. A single model trained on pooled, multicentric, multi-procedural data generalises at least as well as and often better than centre- or procedure-specific models for phase recognition in MIS-CRS, while procedure-specific step models retain advantages for certain procedure types, motivating hybrid training strategies for future surgical AI development.

</details>


### [56] [G3Ego: Gaze-Guided Graphs for Egocentric Action Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20157)
*Marko Haralović,Akash Ramakrishnan,Estefania Talavera Martinez*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: G3Ego uses gaze-guided graphs for egocentric action understanding, constructing and pruning a scene graph from sparse frames using vision-language descriptions, grounded objects, and hand cues, then temporally aggregating graph embeddings for action recognition and anticipation; achieves competitive results without heavy video pretraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Egocentric actions hinge on limited hand–object interactions; existing models rely on exocentric data or generic attention; there is a need for an efficient, interpretable representation that focuses on action-relevant entities guided by gaze.

Method: From sparsely sampled frames, build action scene graphs via vision-language descriptions, grounding objects, and hand cues; prune irrelevant entities using the wearer's gaze; temporally aggregate graph embeddings for recognition and anticipation.

Result: On EGTEA Gaze+ and MECCANO, G3Ego is competitive with video-based approaches and yields consistent Macro-F1 gains under class imbalance; it also avoids expensive video pretraining.

Conclusion: Gaze-guided graph representations provide effective, efficient, and interpretable egocentric action understanding by explicitly encoding action-relevant interactions.

Abstract: Egocentric action understanding is often addressed using large video models pretrained on extensive exocentric datasets. However, many first-person actions depend on a small number of hand-object interactions involving only a few relevant entities.
  We propose G3Ego, a graph-based framework for egocentric action understanding that uses gaze as a structural cue to identify action-relevant entities in the scene. From sparsely sampled frames, G3Ego constructs action scene graphs from vision-language descriptions, grounded objects, and hand cues, and then prunes irrelevant entities using the camera wearer's gaze.
  The resulting graph embeddings are temporally aggregated for action recognition and anticipation. Unlike prior work that uses gaze primarily as an auxiliary modality or attention signal, G3Ego incorporates gaze directly into graph construction, producing efficient and interpretable representations focused on action-relevant interactions.
  Experiments on EGTEA Gaze+ and MECCANO show that G3Ego achieves competitive performance compared with video-based approaches and consistently improves Macro-F1 under class-imbalanced evaluation, while avoiding reliance on computationally expensive video pretraining. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of gaze-guided graph representations for egocentric action understanding.

</details>


### [57] [RoMAN-Flow: Taming Autoregressive Normalizing Flows for Offline Reinforcement Learning in Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20208)
*Shaoxuan Wang,Guangting Zheng,Rui Huang,Zhipeng Tang,Sha Zhang,Jiajun Deng,Yanyong Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RoMAN-Flow makes autoregressive normalizing-flow policies practical for offline RL in robotic manipulation by eliminating sampling during training via an advantage-weighted likelihood objective and by distilling to a one-step action generator for fast deployment, achieving competitive performance with lower latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Offline RL with expressive AR-NF policies offers exact likelihoods but suffers from sampling overhead during both policy optimization and deployment. There is a need for likelihood-based offline RL that is computationally efficient for real-time robotic manipulation.

Method: Use a sampling-free, advantage-weighted likelihood objective during policy optimization to favor high-advantage actions from the offline dataset without sampling from the autoregressive policy. Then distill the optimized AR-NF policy into a one-step action generator to enable low-latency deployment.

Result: Experiments across multiple simulated manipulation benchmarks and real-world robotic platforms show competitive policy performance with substantially reduced inference latency.

Conclusion: RoMAN-Flow provides a practical AR-NF offline RL framework for robotic manipulation by removing the sampling bottleneck in optimization and deployment while maintaining competitive performance.

Abstract: Offline reinforcement learning improves robotic policies using previously collected data without further environment interaction. Yet prevalent diffusion- and flow-matching robot policies lack tractable likelihoods, limiting their use in likelihood-based offline RL post-training. AR-NFs offer both expressive action modeling and exact likelihood evaluation, but their sequential sampling incurs substantial sampling overhead during policy optimization and deployment. We present RoMAN-Flow (Robotic Manipulation with Autoregressive Normalizing Flows), an offline reinforcement learning framework that makes AR-NF policies practical for robotic manipulation by addressing this sampling bottleneck in both stages. During policy optimization, RoMAN-Flow employs a sampling-free, advantage-weighted likelihood objective that assigns higher likelihood to high-advantage actions from the offline dataset without sampling from the autoregressive policy. For efficient deployment, it distills the optimized autoregressive policy into a one-step action generator, enabling low-latency action prediction. Experiments across multiple simulated manipulation benchmarks and real-world robotic platforms demonstrate that RoMAN-Flow achieves competitive policy performance while substantially reducing inference latency. Code is available at https://github.com/konnyaku28/RoMAN-Flow.

</details>


### [58] [Unwarping the Lens: A Physics-Grounded Approach to Video Glasses Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20212)
*Radim Spetlik,David Futschik,Radek Danecek,Feitong Tan,Ziqian Bai,Rohit Pandey,Yinda Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes JFSnet, a transfer framework that uses synthetic data from Nano Banana/Gemini 3 Pro Image, three-stage structural filtering, and lens-optics simulation to train a restoration model for eyeglasses removal with high fidelity and temporal consistency, achieving 27.68 FPS and outperforming diffusion/GAN baselines in perceptual studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Eyeglasses distort facial geometry with refractive and view-dependent reflections, causing identity drift when removing glasses with priors trained on static images. A structurally constrained, temporally consistent approach is needed to preserve identity, expression, and pose in both images and video.

Method: Generate high-fidelity synthetic faces from a commercial generative model; apply a three-stage structural filtering to preserve identity, expression, and pose; simulate lens optics during training to create paired data; train JFSnet, which fuses DINOv2 semantic features with a convolutional decoder and enforces translation equivariance for temporal consistency; operate as a fast inference pipeline (~27.68 FPS).

Result: On FFHQ (12,163 images), the method attains high fidelity and structural accuracy; in CelebV-Text video evaluations, it is consistently preferred over diffusion and GAN baselines for ocular consistency, temporal stability, and overall restoration quality.

Conclusion: Transferring photo-realistic, multi-view knowledge from a strong generative prior into a dedicated restoration architecture via synthetic, physics-informed data yields robust eyeglasses removal with strong identity preservation and temporal coherence, while maintaining real-time-like performance.

Abstract: High-fidelity removal of eyeglasses from video is a major challenge in facial attribute editing, as the underlying facial geometry is often obscured by complex refractive distortions and view-dependent specular reflections. While large-scale generative priors have shown promise in eye-glasses removal via static image inpainting, they often lack the structural constraints necessary to maintain identity, expression, and pose, leading to visible "identity drift" in both static images and dynamic sequences. In this paper, we propose a novel transfer framework that addresses the stochastic nature of generative priors. Our pipeline first extracts high-fidelity synthetic face images from a commercial-grade generative model (Nano Banana, Gemini 3 Pro Image), regularizes them via a three-stage structural filtering process to preserve identity, expression, and pose, and finally applies physically-based simulation of lens optics during training to provide diverse, paired data. This process transfers Nano Banana's photo-realistic, multi-view knowledge into a specialized restoration architecture, JFSnet (Joint Feature-Spatial network). JFSnet integrates DINOv2-based semantic features with a convolutional decoder for spatial reconstruction, leveraging translation equivariance constraints to improve temporal consistency and high-frequency detail preservation. Evaluations on the curated Flickr-Faces-HQ (FFHQ) subset (12,163 images) show that our approach achieves high fidelity and structural accuracy, while maintaining inference speed of 27.68 FPS. In perceptual studies on CelebV-Text video sequences, our results are consistently preferred over diffusion and GAN-based baselines for ocular consistency, temporal stability, and overall restoration quality.

</details>


### [59] [Prompt-Conditioned Channel Attention for Hierarchical Feature Modulation toward Anatomy-Agnostic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20229)
*Mosharof Hossain,Md Rabiul Islam,Limon Halder,Erchin Serpedin,Md Kamrul Hasan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Prompt-Conditioned Channel Attention (PCCA) to enable prompt-driven, multi-scale feature modulation in encoder-decoder networks; yields PROMISE-CNN and PROMISE-Txformer, with consistent IoU gains across four datasets compared to U-Net/UNETR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome late-stage prompt fusion and the lack of explicit, across-scale, channel-wise prompt modulation that can capture deeper contextual and modality-specific variations in medical image segmentation.

Method: PCCA extracts compact channel descriptors via pooling, projects them into a shared space, and fuses them through a gated excitation mechanism to compute prompt-aware channel attention weights for recalibrating features across multiple network stages. Two instantiations are proposed: PROMISE-CNN (CNN-based) and PROMISE-Txformer (transformer-based).

Result: Across ISIC-Lesion, Kvasir-Polyp, CAMUS-Cardiac, and Kvasir-Instrument benchmarks, PROMISE-CNN with PCCA yields relative IoU gains of 10.4%, 8.7%, 0.8%, and 3.4% over U-Net; PROMISE-Txformer yields gains of 7.6%, 23.0%, 2.1%, and 1.1% over UNETR.

Conclusion: PCCA and PROMISE-Net provide a scalable, generalizable framework for prompt-aware hierarchical feature modulation in medical image segmentation.

Abstract: Anatomically plausible segmentation remains challenging because of low contrast, ambiguous boundaries, and modality-specific artifacts. Interactive segmentation has emerged as a promising strategy to guide feature extraction and improve localization, particularly in structurally ambiguous regions. However, existing methods integrate prompts through late-stage fusion and lack explicit mechanisms for prompt-driven channel-wise modulation across hierarchical feature representations, limiting their ability to capture deeper contextual and modality-specific variations. To address these limitations, we introduce Prompt-Conditioned Channel Attention (PCCA), a novel modulation mechanism that enables deep, hierarchical integration of semantic prompts within encoder-decoder networks. PCCA extracts compact channel descriptors via pooling, projects them into a shared space, and fuses them through a gated excitation mechanism to compute prompt-aware channel attention weights. These weights adaptively recalibrate feature responses across multiple network stages, enabling prompt-conditioned, semantically enriched hierarchical representations. Building on this, we propose PROMISE-Net, instantiated in two network variants: a convolutional model (PROMISE-CNN) and a transformer-based model (PROMISE-Txformer). Across the ISIC-Lesion, Kvasir-Polyp, CAMUS-Cardiac, and Kvasir-Instrument benchmarks, integrating PCCA into PROMISE-CNN yielded relative IoU gains of 10.4%, 8.7%, 0.8%, and 3.4%, respectively, over the baseline U-Net, while PROMISE-Txformer achieved corresponding gains of 7.6%, 23.0%, 2.1%, and 1.1%, respectively, over the baseline UNETR. These results show consistent improvements across architectures, imaging modalities, and anatomical targets, establishing PCCA and PROMISE-Net as a scalable, generalizable framework for prompt-aware hierarchical feature modulation in medical image segmentation.

</details>


### [60] [Ultra-High-Definition Restoration Transformers with Correlation Matching Transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20263)
*Cong Wang,Liyan Wang,Jinshan Pan,Wei Wang,Wenqi Ren,Jun Liu,Xiaochun Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UHDformer++ is a Transformer-based framework for UHD image restoration that operates across four coordinated learning spaces (HR, LR, SR, LHFR) and employs two bridging modules (FR-CMT and ACM) to achieve large parameter efficiency (~86% reduction) and strong performance across five UHD restoration tasks (low-light, dehazing, deblurring, deraining, desnowing).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable unified, efficient restoration of ultra-high-definition images by leveraging multi-space feature extraction and cross-space fusion, while substantially reducing model size.

Method: Four learning spaces: (1) High-resolution (HR) for multi-scale features and residuals; (2) Low-resolution (LR) for compact representations; (3) Super-resolution (SR) to upsample LR features; (4) Low-high fusion and reconstruction (LHFR) for final restoration. A cross-space bridge includes FR-CMT (selects top C/r channels from fusion of max- and mean-pooled HR features to replace less informative LR channels) and ACM (adaptive recalibration of multi-scale HR features to feed LR).

Result: Extensive experiments report at least 86% parameter reduction compared to recent SOTA methods, with substantial gains across five UHD restoration tasks.

Conclusion: The approach is a general, task-agnostic framework for UHD image restoration, combining multi-space fusion and channel-wise feature modulation to achieve both efficiency and accuracy; code will be released.

Abstract: We propose UHDformer++, a general Transformer-based framework to solve numerous Ultra-High-Definition (UHD) image restoration tasks. UHDformer++ operates across $4$ coordinated learning spaces: 1) a high-resolution space (HR) for multi-level feature extraction, 2) a low-resolution space (LR) for learning compact, representative features, 3) a super-resolution space (SR) for upsampling low-resolution features from SR, and 4) a low-high fusion and reconstruction space (LHFR) for final image restoration. Specifically, HR extracts multi-scale high-resolution features and fuses them with low-resolution cues to produce residual images, while LR distills complementary representations from HR to improve restoration quality. To supply LHFR with richer features, SR super-resolves LR outputs before fusion. We further introduce two modules to bridge the high- and low-resolution spaces. The Feature-Refined Correlation Matching Transformation (FR-CMT) module selects the top $C/r~(C~\text{denotes the number of channels;~}r\geq1~\text{controls the squeezing level})$, from the fusion between max- and mean-pooled high-resolution features to replace less informative channels in the low-resolution Transformer. The Adaptive Channel Modulator (ACM) adaptively recalibrates multi-scale high-resolution features, ensuring that only task-relevant information propagates to LR. Extensive experiments demonstrate that UHDformer++ reduces model parameters by at least 86\% compared with recent state-of-the-art methods while achieving substantial performance gains across $5$ UHD restoration tasks, including low-light image enhancement, dehazing, deblurring, deraining, and desnowing. Code will be released at https://github.com/supersupercong/uhdformerplus.

</details>


### [61] [CalcSeg: Confidence-aware 3D Latent Context Curriculum Learning For Myocardial Scar Segmentation From Single-Stack LGE-CMRs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20305)
*Nivetha Jayakumar,Hannah Kim,Amit R. Patel,Miaomiao Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CalcSeg introduces a confidence-aware latent context curriculum learning framework for robust myocardial scar segmentation from single-stack LGE-CMR, integrating a dynamic semi-supervised curriculum with a confidence scoring function and latent slice-wise self-attention to infer 3D context from 2D inputs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scar segmentation on single-stack LGE-CMR suffers from low tissue contrast, diffuse/small scars, and limited 3D context; there is a need for robust methods that leverage 3D context and automatically assess sample difficulty to improve learning.

Method: A dynamic semi-supervised curriculum learning strategy using a learned confidence-aware scoring function that combines prediction errors, epistemic uncertainty, and scar burden to order training samples from easy to hard. Additionally, a latent slice-wise self-attention module captures inter-slice dependencies to synthesize 3D spatial representations from sparse 2D stacks. The approach is evaluated on multi-center LGE-CMR datasets and compared against existing scar segmentation networks.

Result: CalcSeg consistently outperforms competing methods, with marked improvements on clinically challenging cases and good generalization across center datasets.

Conclusion: The proposed confidence-aware curriculum and latent context attention enable robust single-stack LGE-CMR scar segmentation by effectively leveraging limited 3D context and automatically adapting training emphasis to sample difficulty; code is released on GitHub.

Abstract: Myocardial scar segmentation from single-stack late gadolinium-enhanced cardiac magnetic resonance (LGE-CMR) imaging has been a longstanding and clinically important challenge, particularly in the presence of low tissue contrast, diffuse, and small scar regions. These challenges are further intensified by the limited availability of 3D spatial context. This paper presents CalcSeg, a Confidence-aware latent context curriculum learning framework that leverages fused 3D feature representations from single-stack 2D LGE-CMR images for robust scar segmentation. Specifically, we introduce a dynamic semi-supervised curriculum learning strategy that progressively expands training from easier to more challenging scar cases using a learned confidence-aware scoring function. Such a function integrates errors in the predicted scar maps with quantified epistemic uncertainty and scar burden estimation to automatically assess sample difficulty without requiring manual labels. To compensate for the limited spatial context in single-stack acquisitions, we then develop a latent slice-wise self-attention to capture inter-slice dependencies and infer 3D spatial representations from sparse 2D inputs. We evaluate CalcSeg on multi-center clinical LGE-CMR datasets and benchmark against existing scar segmentation networks. Experimental results show that CalcSeg consistently outperforms all competing methods, particularly with substantial improvements on clinically challenging cases. Our code is released on Github.

</details>


### [62] [DreamHand: Repurposing Video Diffusion Models for Occlusion-Robust Egocentric 3D Hand Motion Recovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20308)
*Yufei Liu,Xixi Wang,Hao Li,Ganlong Zhao,Kaitong Cai,Chengkai Jin,Chunxiao Liu,Jianbo Liu,Siyuan Huang,Xingang Pan,Hongsheng Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DreamHand uses a deterministic clean-latent encoder derived from a diffusion model to recover metric 3D hand trajectories from egocentric video, via a clip-level offline framework with a bidirectional spatiotemporal decoder; achieves state-of-the-art on occlusion-heavy benchmarks without test-time camera intrinsics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recover metric 3D hand trajectories from egocentric video despite severe occlusion and out-of-sight gaps. Existing single-frame/windowed methods fail when hands exit frame, and diffusion models are too heavy; a deterministic, single-pass, geometry-aware approach can exploit latent scene information beyond current observations.

Method: Repurpose a video diffusion model as a deterministic geometry encoder that, after a single forward pass of the clean latent, reveals content beyond current observations. DreamHand is an offline clip-level framework using a Deterministic Clean-Latent Encoder to extract features and a Bidirectional Spatiotemporal Decoder to reconstruct continuous, metric hand trajectories. A Ray-Based Camera Solver enables a second configuration that does not require test-time intrinsics.

Result: Across five egocentric benchmarks, DreamHand achieves state-of-the-art performance, reducing MPJPE-p by 30% on occlusion-heavy ARCTIC and 40% on HOT3D; gains reach 46%-61% when including out-of-sight hands in evaluation.

Conclusion: DreamHand demonstrates a scalable path from everyday egocentric video to robot manipulation data by leveraging a deterministic diffusion-based geometry encoder, removing the need for external detectors and offering configurations with or without test-time camera intrinsics.

Abstract: Egocentric video offers scalable manipulation data for embodied AI, yet recovering metric 3D hand trajectories remains challenging due to severe object occlusion and frequent out-of-sight gaps. Existing single-frame and windowed temporal regressors fail when hand shortly leaves the frame, while recent video diffusion models (VDMs) rely on heavy, stochastic multi-step sampling as pixel-space renderers. We instead repurpose VDM into a deterministic geometry encoder. A single forward pass over the clean latent exposes scene content beyond current observations, including occluded and out-of-sight hands. We introduce DreamHand, an offline clip-level framework that extracts features via a Deterministic Clean-Latent Encoder and decodes them with a Bidirectional Spatiotemporal Decoder. DreamHand recovers continuous bimanual trajectories with metric placement and no external detector, while a Ray-Based Camera Solver supports a second configuration that needs no test-time camera intrinsics. Across five egocentric benchmarks, DreamHand sets a new state of the art, cutting MPJPE-p by 30% on occlusion-heavy ARCTIC and 40% on HOT3D. These gains reach 46%-61% once out-of-sight hands are included in the evaluation, offering a scalable path from everyday human video to robot manipulation data.

</details>


### [63] [Inter-X++: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Multimodal Human-Human Interaction Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20312)
*Liang Xu,Chengqun Yang,Zili Lin,Xintao Lv,Yichao Yan,Xin Jin,Zhibo Chen,Xiaokang Yang,Wenjun Zeng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A large-scale, unified benchmark and modeling framework for human-human interaction (HHI) analysis, featuring Inter-X++ (11k sequences, 8.1M frames, full-body and finger kinematics with rich multimodal annotations) and OpenHHI (joint reconstruction and semantic understanding) that establishes standardized tasks and evaluation for generation and perception tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current HHI datasets suffer from low-fidelity kinematics, missing dexterous hand gestures, and fragmented annotations with inconsistent evaluation protocols, hindering fair benchmarking and progress in both perception and generation tasks.

Method: Inter-X++ collects high-fidelity interaction data using a novel hybrid motion capture system, providing extensive annotations (textual descriptions, interaction categories, causal orders, subject relationships/personality, vertex-level contact maps, and physically regularized constraints). It defines four downstream tasks spanning generative and perceptive paradigms with standardized interaction representations and evaluation protocols. OpenHHI introduces a unified HHI representation and modeling framework that jointly optimizes interaction reconstruction and semantic understanding.

Result: Demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on both generation and perception tasks on the proposed benchmarks, validating that the unified representation can bridge interaction understanding and generation.

Conclusion: Inter-X++ and OpenHHI together offer a comprehensive dataset and a unified modeling framework that enable rigorous, cross-task HHI analysis and benchmarking, advancing both perception and generation of human-human interactions.

Abstract: The capability to perceive and synthesize human-human interactions is fundamental to developing intelligent digital human systems. However, existing datasets and modeling approaches are fundamentally constrained by low-fidelity kinematics, the omission of dexterous hand gestures and a severe lack of rich multimodal annotations. Furthermore, fragmented interaction representations and inconsistent evaluation protocols also impede fair and rigorous benchmarking. To systematically address these bottlenecks, we present Inter-X++, a comprehensive and large-scale benchmark designed to empower versatile HHI analysis. Captured via a novel hybrid motion capture system, Inter-X++ provides 11,388 high-fidelity interaction sequences and over 8.1M frames, featuring precise whole-body movements and detailed finger articulations. Meanwhile, we enrich the data foundation with multifaceted annotations, including hierarchical fine-grained textual descriptions, interaction categories, causal interaction orders, the relationship and personality of the subjects, as well as vertex-level contact maps and physically regularized constraints. Leveraging these elaborate annotations, we formulate a unified testing ground comprising four categories of downstream tasks that symmetrically span both generative and perceptive paradigms. To eliminate benchmarking ambiguities, we systematically standardize the interaction representations and evaluation protocols. Finally, we go beyond dataset construction to propose OpenHHI, a single and unified HHI representation and modeling framework that jointly optimizes interaction reconstruction and semantic understanding. Extensive experiments reveal that OpenHHI achieves state-of-the-art performance on both generation and perception tasks. This definitively proves that our unified representation successfully bridges interaction understanding and generation simultaneously.

</details>


### [64] [Swift-Image: Exploring the Performance Frontier of Compact Unified Image Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20334)
*Taihang Hu,Zhao Wang,Zuan Gao,Tao Liu,Hao Yan,Zhengze Xu,Yuhang Yu,Yongchao Du,Xingjian Wang,Jun Zheng,Qinye Zhou,Zhengrui Chen,Chao Lin,Yefeng Shen,Zhengtao Wu,Ge Wu,Xiaoli Xu,Denghui Yang,Huayu Zhang,Mingzhou Zhang,Mengting Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A compact 6B single-stream DiT model (Swift-Image) for text-to-image generation and editing, employing progressive training, post-training distillation, and a Prompt Enhancer to achieve competitive performance with 3B and accelerated variants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Investigate how far a small visual generator can be pushed under constrained compute, via curriculum-driven training and unified generation-editing supervision.

Method: 6B DiT single-stream; progressive training from broad semantic coverage to high-resolution visuals; parallel expert RL and multi-teacher on-policy distillation; Prompt Enhancer translating requests into generator-aligned visual specs; structural pruning to 3B; few-step distillation for faster sampling.

Result: Leads among evaluated open-source models with 6B parameters and 243K GPU hours; 3B compressed model incurs negligible performance loss; few-step distillation improves aggregate editing performance with substantially fewer sampling steps.

Conclusion: Provides practical lessons on architecture, data curriculum, post-training, prompt enhancement, and model compression for compact unified generation-editing models.

Abstract: We present Swift-Image, a compact unified model for text-to-image generation, single-image editing, and multi-image editing. Our goal is to explore how far a relatively small visual generator can be pushed through systematic training engineering under a constrained computational budget. Swift-Image adopts an efficient 6B single-stream DiT and a progressive training pipeline that evolves from broad semantic coverage to higher resolution, stronger visual quality, and unified generation-editing supervision. For post-training, we employ parallel expert reinforcement learning followed by multi-teacher on-policy distillation to alleviate interference among heterogeneous objectives. We further decouple high-level reasoning from pixel-level rendering with a Prompt Enhancer that translates user requests into generator-aligned visual specifications. For efficient deployment, structural pruning and few-step distillation produce 3B and accelerated variants. Swift-Image achieves leading aggregate performance among evaluated open-source models with only 6B parameters and 243K GPU training hours; the compressed 3B model incurs nearly no loss, while few-step distillation further improves aggregate editing performance with substantially fewer sampling steps. Our study also summarizes practical lessons for architecture, data curriculum, post-training, prompt enhancement, and model compression.

</details>


### [65] [4DAnyone: Create Anyone in 4D from a Casual Monocular Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20335)
*Yudong Jin,Tao Xie,Qihang Zhang,Zehong Shen,Zhen Xu,Yujun Shen,Hujun Bao,Xiaowei Zhou,Yinghao Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 4DAnyone: a framework for reconstructing 4D humans from monocular video by producing multiview-consistent videos and lifting to 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS). It tackles scaling challenges (bounded-attention-context) with Reference Context Packing and Target Context Routing, introduces the MVGameHuman dataset, and reports state-of-the-art novel-view video quality and 4DGS reconstruction with strong generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Camera-controlled video diffusion models struggle to maintain cross-view consistency when the number of target views grows to the tens required for 4DGS reconstruction; current approaches face two coupled bottlenecks that degrade reconstruction quality.

Method: Introduce two complementary designs: (1) Reference Context Packing (RCP) to compress growing reference views into a fixed-length, mixed-resolution context with O(1) reference-context complexity; (2) Target Context Routing (TCR) to rotate target-view groupings during denoising so context is shared across groups at high-noise steps and stabilizes details at low-noise steps. Build the MVGameHuman dataset using an in-house game engine, and train on MVGameHuman plus light-stage and in-the-wild video data. Reconstruct 4D humans via lifting to 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS).

Result: 4DAnyone outperforms prior methods in both novel-view video quality and downstream 4DGS reconstruction, with robust generalization to in-the-wild data; validated on DNA-Rendering and DyMVHumans.

Conclusion: The framework effectively resolves bounded-attention-context bottlenecks, enabling scalable, consistent cross-view synthesis and accurate 4DGS reconstruction from monocular video; provides the MVGameHuman dataset and open-source resources.

Abstract: We present 4DAnyone, a framework for reconstructing 4D humans from an uncalibrated monocular video by generating reconstruction-grade multiview-consistent videos and lifting them into 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS). Existing camera-controlled video diffusion models synthesize plausible novel-view videos but fail to maintain consistency when scaled to the tens of target views required for 4DGS reconstruction. We identify this failure as a bounded-attention-context problem: when target views exceed the capacity of a single DiT forward pass, they must be split into groups, exposing two coupled bottlenecks. On the reference-context side, conditioning on all previously generated views grows as $O(N)$, weakening cross-view appearance guidance. On the target-context side, disjoint groups cannot directly exchange information, causing global structural drift. 4DAnyone addresses both bottlenecks with two complementary designs: Reference Context Packing (RCP) compresses growing reference views into a fixed-length mixed-resolution context with $O(1)$ reference-context complexity, while Target Context Routing (TCR) rotates target-view groupings during denoising to share context across groups at high-noise steps and stabilize details at low-noise steps. We further build the MVGameHuman dataset using our in-house game engine and combine it with light-stage and in-the-wild video datasets for training. Experiments on DNA-Rendering and DyMVHumans show that 4DAnyone outperforms prior methods in both novel-view video quality and downstream 4DGS reconstruction, with robust in-the-wild generalization. See our project page for video results and source code: https://4danyone.github.io.

</details>


### [66] [WithEveryone: Unified Planning and Identity Grounding for Group Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20336)
*Hengyuan Xu,Qixun Wang,Yiji Cheng,Miles Yang,Zhao Zhong,Wei Cheng,Xingjun Ma,Yu-gang Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: WithEveryone is a framework for generating group images with up to ten reference identities by injecting identities as tokens, predicting a layout plan, and grounding identity losses to avoid unstable face matching, achieving high identity preservation with low copy-paste artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Identity-preserving multi-person generation is hard when many specified people must be present; existing embedding-based face matching is unstable and does not scale well; a layout-grounded approach is needed to bind references to distinct individuals and locations.

Method: Introduce WithEveryone that injects each identity as an addressed token, predicts a structured identity-layout plan, and renders it as a visual condition. Uses Layout-Grounded ID Loss supervised by annotated face regions to directly supervise identity assignment, and ID Representation Forcing to predict each identity before synthesis. Evaluated on an identity-disjoint benchmark.

Result: Outperforms baselines in target-context identity similarity (0.499 vs 0.462) and reduces copy-paste artifacts (0.055 vs 0.169); covers 97.3% of requested identities with 2.8% duplicates, enabling scalable identity-preserving generation without direct face copying.

Conclusion: Explicit identity-layout grounding enables scalable, high-fidelity identity-preserving group generation for multiple identities, reducing reliance on reference-face copying and improving alignment between identities and their layout.

Abstract: Identity-preserving image generation becomes increasingly unreliable when a scene must contain many specified people. Beyond retaining each identity, the model must bind every reference to a distinct person and location, while training-time identity losses must establish correspondence among several noisy predicted faces. We introduce WithEveryone, a unified framework for generating group images up to ten reference identities. WithEveryone injects each selected identity as an addressed token, predicts a structured identity--layout plan, and renders the plan as a visual condition. Its key objective, Layout-Grounded ID Loss, uses annotated face regions to supervise the intended identities directly, avoiding unstable embedding-based face matching; ID Representation Forcing additionally trains a prediction for each identity before image synthesis. On an identity-disjoint benchmark, WithEveryone achieves the highest target-context identity similarity, improving face similarity from 0.462 for GPT-Image-2 to 0.499, while reducing copy-paste artifacts from 0.169 to 0.055. It further covers 97.3\% of the requested identities with a duplicate rate of only 2.8\%. These results show that explicit identity--layout grounding enables identity-preserving generation to scale to larger groups without relying on direct reference-face copying.

</details>


<div id='cs.CG'></div>

### [67] [Polyomino Nets Covering Three Different Boxes of Area 106 and Related Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19910)
*Erik D. Demaine,Jenny Diomidova,Nicole Jacobus,Landon Kryger,Matthew T. Parker,Michael Tardibuono,Ryuhei Uehara,Hanyu Alice Zhang*

Main category: cs.CG

TL;DR: Computational discovery of polyomino nets that fold into three distinct cuboids with equal surface area (106): 40 nets that fold into all three, infinite families of such nets, and counts of common nets among smaller cuboids; includes algorithmic innovations and open questions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Advance understanding of polyomino nets and their multi-cuboid foldability; contribute enumerative results and methodological tools for searching nets.

Method: Computer search leveraging hidden structures in Nx1x1-net shapes, striped folding patterns, and a variant of Redelmeier's algorithm; enumeration of nets folding into three cuboids; analysis of infinite families and cross-counts of nets.

Result: Found 40 nets that fold into all three cuboids (the three with surface area 106), established infinite families of triple-folding nets, and computed the number of nets common to smaller cuboids; explained the search algorithms and structural observations.

Conclusion: Proposes open questions to broaden the theoretical and computational understanding of polyomino nets and their multi-cuboid foldability.

Abstract: We present new results for polyomino nets that fold into 2 and 3 different cuboids through a computer search. The main result is the finding of 40 nets that fold into all three different cuboids with a surface area of 106. The secondary results are the finding of infinite families of nets that fold into three cuboid shapes, and the calculation of the number of common nets between smaller cuboids. The algorithms used to make the searches feasible will also be explained. The algorithms include taking advantage of some hidden structures in the nets that fold into the Nx1x1 cuboids, taking advantage of how a lot of nets fold into cuboid shapes in a 'striped' way, and using a variant of Redelmeier's algorithm. The paper ends with open questions that encourage the reader to broaden our collective understanding of the subject of creating polyomino nets.

</details>


<div id='cs.LG'></div>

# cs.LG [[Back]](#toc)

### [68] [Towards On-Board Implementation of ML-Based Helicopter Weight Estimator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19210)
*Nicolas Valot,Ammar Mechouche,Benjamin Lesage,Claire Pagetti,Louis Fabre*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A supervised ML model (LSTM) estimates helicopter takeoff weight using Airbus fleet data; aligned with ML assurance standards (EASA concept paper and Eurocae ED-324); implemented on legacy avionics for potential onboard alerting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate weight estimation at takeoff is critical for performance, safety, and operational efficiency. The work seeks to establish a compliant ML development lifecycle and assurance process for aviation applications, leveraging a large, real-world in-service dataset.

Method: Develop an LSTM-based regression model to estimate weight. Define a machine-learning requirements framework and a model description aligned with EASA and ED-324 guidance. Implement the model on legacy avionics computers and verify the requirements against the implementation. Train/evaluate using Airbus fleet data and assess deployment feasibility for airborne targets and critical functions such as on-board alerting.

Result: The authors verify the ML requirements on the implementation and demonstrate that the weight estimator can be deployed on legacy airborne hardware, with potential use in onboard alerting and other critical functions.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates a feasible, standards-aligned ML weight estimator for helicopters, illustrating end-to-end development, verification, and deployment considerations for avionics-grade ML on legacy hardware.

Abstract: This paper focuses on the implementation of a novel supervised Machine Learning model for estimating helicopter weight during takeoff, utilizing extensive datasets from Airbus's global in-service fleet. The study details a learning assurance process aligned with the EASA concept paper for machine learning application, and with the on-going Eurocae ED-324. We propose a set of Machine Learning Requirements, a Machine Learning Model Description, and its implementation for a long short-term memory recurrent neural network. Finally, we verify the requirements on the implementation. Demonstrated on legacy avionics computers, the implementation is suitable for the deployment of the developed Machine Learning Model weight estimator on airborne targets for critical functions such as on-board alerting.

</details>


### [69] [Triangular Fuzzy Rescaling Distance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19234)
*Eddy Soria,Aida Valls,Ana Beatriz Hernández-Lara*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A new distance metric d_TR for Triangular Fuzzy Numbers integrates Linear Rescaling into the distance computation, yielding a metric that is bounded, scale-invariant, and origin-invariant, suitable for heterogeneous fuzzy data with a weighting vector for multi-attribute contexts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fuzzy distance measures often assume same-scale attributes; heterogeneity in data necessitates normalization that can distort comparisons. A metric that inherently handles rescaling during distance computation addresses this gap.

Method: Define Triangular Fuzzy Rescaling Distance (d_TR) by embedding Linear Rescaling (LRE) into the distance calculation between TFNs; prove metric properties (non-negativity, identity, symmetry, triangle inequality); show d_TR is bounded, scale-invariant, origin-invariant; incorporate a weighting vector for dimensions.

Result: Proves d_TR satisfies metric axioms; demonstrates boundedness, scale-invariance, origin-invariance; shows applicability to synthetic indicators, distance-based ML, and multicriteria decision aiding.

Conclusion: d_TR provides a robust, normalization-free distance for heterogeneous fuzzy data, enabling reliable comparisons of TFNs in complex decision problems and learning tasks.

Abstract: Decision-making in complex systems often involves dealing with imprecise or uncertain information, frequently represented using fuzzy sets, particularly Triangular Fuzzy Numbers (TFNs). A crucial aspect of many fuzzy methods is the quantification of distance between TFNs. Many distance measures assume that all values are in the same scale, requiring a preliminary normalization stage when applied to heterogeneous attributes with different scales or units. This paper proposes the Triangular Fuzzy Rescaling Distance (d_{TR}), a metric designed to address this challenge. The d_{TR} uniquely integrates Linear Rescaling (LRE) directly into the distance calculation, ensuring normalization during the comparison of fuzzy numbers. We formally prove that d_{TR} satisfies the properties of a metric, including non-negativity, identity, symmetry, and the triangle inequality. Furthermore, we demonstrate that d_{TR} is bounded, scale-invariant, and origin-invariant. These properties, combined with a weighting vector for prioritizing dimensions, make d_{TR} suitable for applications involving heterogeneous fuzzy data, such as the construction of synthetic indicators, distance-based machine learning algorithms or multicriteria-decision aiding.

</details>


### [70] [Holtercare-Bench: A Multimodal Benchmark for Evaluating Long-Term Dynamic ECG Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19297)
*Yihan Xie,Hanwen Cui,Runze Ye,Juekai Lin,Haoyang Wang,Jinhao Mao,Bo Zhang,Wenqiao Zhang,Xiaogang Guo,Jun Xiao,Lei Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Introduces Holtercare-23K, a large-scale multimodal dynamic ECG dataset with 22,980 QA pairs from 788 Holter records and a tri-modal signal-video-text alignment, and Holtercare-Bench, a benchmark for temporal localization, clinical diagnosis, and summarization. Zero-shot MLLMs reveal gaps in processing ultra-long pathological sequences; fine-tuning improves performance. Provides a foundation for long-term medical MLLMs in electrophysiology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is a need for high-quality, long-term multimodal datasets and benchmarks tailored to dynamic ECG and electrophysiology to support temporal reasoning and comprehensive report generation in medical AI.

Method: Create Holtercare-23K: a large-scale multimodal dynamic ECG dataset with signal-video-text alignment and 22,980 QA pairs from 788 clinical Holter records. Develop Holtercare-Bench to evaluate models on temporal localization, clinical diagnosis, and global summarization. Conduct zero-shot evaluations on leading MLLMs and fine-tune representative models. Provide project at the given GitHub repo.

Result: Holtercare-23K provides 22,980 QA pairs from 788 records with tri-modal alignment. Holtercare-Bench enables evaluation across temporal localization, diagnosis, and summarization. Zero-shot MLLMs show significant performance gaps on ultra-long sequences; fine-tuning yields substantial improvements.

Conclusion: The work fills a gap in electrophysiology AI by offering a dedicated long-term multimodal ECG dataset and benchmark, establishing a foundation for future long-term medical MLLMs; the authors provide a public resource to accelerate research.

Abstract: While multimodal large language models (MLLMs) excel in medical applications, most of them favor static images or short-term signals. In the critical field of dynamic electrocardiograms (ECG), models struggle with complex temporal reasoning and diagnostic report generation due to a lack of high-quality datasets and benchmarks. To address this, we introduce (i) Holtercare-23K, a large-scale multimodal dynamic ECG dataset comprising 22,980 QA pairs derived from 788 clinical Holter records and featuring a novel signal-video-text tri-modal alignment. Based on this dataset, we present (ii) Holtercare-Bench, a multimodal benchmark that evaluates models on temporal localization, clinical diagnosis, and global summarization. Zero-shot evaluations of leading MLLMs reveal a significant performance gap in processing ultra-long pathological sequences. However, fine-tuning representative models yields substantial improvements. This work illuminates the limitations of current MLLMs in electrophysiology and provides a foundational benchmark for long-term medical MLLMs. Our project is available at https://github.com/ZJU4HealthCare/Holtercare-Bench.

</details>


### [71] [Quantum Kernel Estimation for the Discovery of Early Lung Cancer Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19304)
*Hamed Javidi,Alex Zajichek,Hakan Doga,Laxmi Parida,Filippo Utro,Peter J. Mazzone*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Quantum-classical hybrid ML using cfDNA features shows competitive lung cancer detection; 20-feature quantum configurations may improve AUC for fragmentomics; methylation favors classical SVM; 40 features less consistently beneficial; quantum kernels are promising.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lung cancer screening with low-dose CT has uptake/adherence issues; cfDNA biomarkers offer complementary detection; quantum kernel methods may capture nonlinear, high-dimensional cfDNA signals.

Method: Feature selection to 20/40 features; encode features into quantum Hilbert space via angle and dense-angle feature maps with multiple entanglement strategies; compute fidelity-based quantum kernels via exact statevector simulation; integrate with precomputed-kernel SVM and kernel-PCA logistic regression; compare to an SVM trained on original features; held-out evaluation on two datasets.

Result: Quantum-kernel models competitive; fragmentomics: some 20-feature setups improved AUC vs classical SVM; methylation: classical SVM had highest AUC; some quantum models improved specificity; increasing features from 20 to 40 did not consistently help and increased variability.

Conclusion: Quantum kernel methods show promise for cfDNA-based lung cancer detection, with dataset-dependent gains; encoding/entanglement design impact performance; further optimization could enhance effectiveness and generalizability.

Abstract: Lung cancer screening with low-dose chest computed tomography reduces mortality, but its impact is limited by uptake, adherence, and management challenges. Blood-based cell-free DNA (cfDNA) biomarkers offer a complementary approach, although early detection remains difficult because of lung cancer heterogeneity and high-dimensional, nonlinear molecular signals. We evaluated quantum-classical hybrid machine learning for lung cancer detection using DNA fragmentomics and DNA methylation. After feature selection, models were trained using 20- and 40-feature subsets. Features were encoded into quantum Hilbert space using angle and dense-angle feature maps with multiple entanglement strategies. Fidelity-based quantum kernels were computed with exact statevector simulation and integrated with precomputed-kernel SVM and kernel-PCA logistic regression and compared with an SVM model trained on the original features.
  This framework enabled systematic evaluation of how encoding and entanglement design affect classification. Across repeated held-out evaluations, quantum-kernel models achieved competitive performance on both datasets. For fragmentomics, several 20-feature configurations improved AUC relative to a classical SVM baseline, suggesting effective capture of nonlinear cfDNA fragmentation structure. For methylation, the classical SVM achieved the highest AUC, although selected quantum models remained competitive and improved specificity in some cases. Increasing features from 20 to 40 did not consistently improve performance and often increased variability. Overall, these results support quantum kernel methods as a promising approach for cfDNA-based lung cancer detection.

</details>


### [72] [Improved Confidence Estimates for Black-Box Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19323)
*Sokhna Diarra Mbacke,Mouloud Belbahri,Gabriel Loaiza-Ganem*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A lightweight, data-driven post-hoc calibration: train a simple classifier using existing uncertainty scores and neighbor-query correctness to predict answer correctness, achieving better uncertainty estimates with minimal overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Uncertainty quantification (UQ) methods for LLMs often rely on zero-shot scores that may poorly reflect actual correctness; there is a need for dataset-aware, low-cost improvements to UQ that perform well in real deployments.

Method: Train a simple classifier (e.g., logistic regression) that uses as features the zero-shot UQ scores and the correctness of similar (neighbor) queries; use the dataset of interest to supervise learning and predict LLM response correctness.

Result: The learned classifier consistently outperforms baseline UQ scores on the dataset, with minimal additional computational overhead.

Conclusion: Dataset-specific, lightweight post-hoc calibration to UQ yields more accurate predictions of LLM correctness while preserving efficiency, making it suitable for real-world applications.

Abstract: Uncertainty quantification (UQ) is essential for the safe deployment of large language models (LLMs). Existing methods, from verbalized confidence to ones requiring multiple generations, are often zero-shot and produce scores quantifying uncertainty without the need for labelled data. Nonetheless, in practice one must always evaluate their performance on a dataset of interest before deployment. In this work we show that, by leveraging this dataset, we consistently outperform these existing scores. Specifically, we build simple classifiers that predict LLM response correctness by using these scores and the correctness of similar queries as features. Our method produces minimal computational overhead, making it a cheap and straightforward enhancement for UQ in LLMs for real-world applications.

</details>


### [73] [Mechanistic Tomography: Designed Measurement for Control-Oriented Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19338)
*Vijay Erramilli*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A tomography-style framework for mechanistic interpretability: recover internal model mechanisms via linear measurements y = Ax + w, with an iterative, basis- and intervention-aware protocol; demonstrated on GPT-2-small IOI and Qwen-2.5-7B showing model- and task-dependent interactions and measurement choices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Expose and quantify internal, non-directly observable mechanisms, intervention effects, and interactions in neural models; unify patching, gradients, Hessian-vector products under a single, principled measurement framework; enable principled experimental design and validation.

Method: Choose a basis and intervention family; collect measurements y = Ax + w where A encodes interventions and x the target map. Start with low-cost measurements, test on held-out interventions at target scale, calibrate simple mismatches, and expand the measurement family as structured residuals persist. Consider forward-only vs gradient access, use sparse aggregate measurements, lifted measurements, and Hessian-vector products to recover missing interactions; use Tracr to determine required measurement family given the basis; apply to models such as GPT-2-small IOI and Qwen-2.5-7B to analyze cross-model interactions (e.g., Name Mover categories) and additive maps.

Result: Forward-only measurements recover a finite-effect map with relatively few interventions; gradient access yields localized attribution improvements; lifted measurements and Hessian-vector products reveal interactions missed by first-order maps; Tracr shows the necessary measurement family depends on the chosen basis; in experiments, GPT-2-small IOI reveals Name Mover–Negative Name Mover as a dominant held-out term; Qwen-2.5-7B shows an additive refusal–response map suffices, with no extra benefit from pairwise lifting.

Conclusion: Mechanistic tomography provides a practical, validation-driven protocol for probing internal model mechanisms; the optimal measurement strategy depends on the chosen basis and access, and iterative calibration plus expanding the measurement family are key when residuals remain.

Abstract: Mechanistic interpretability seeks quantities that models do not expose directly: represented states, component effects, interactions, and responses to interventions. Patching, gradients, Hessian-vector products, and subset interventions provide different measurements under different access assumptions and may target different quantities. We formulate their shared measurement structure as mechanistic tomography: designed measurement for recovering internal mechanisms and intervention effects.
  For a chosen basis and intervention family, measurements take the form y = Ax + w, where A describes the interventions, x is the target map, and w contains nonlinear response, sampling error, and basis misspecification. This language gives a practical procedure: start with the least costly measurements, test on held-out interventions at the intended scale, calibrate simple mismatch, and expand the measurement family when structured residuals remain.
  Control provides a demanding validation setting because an estimate that guides an intervention acts as an observer. In a two-HMM model, control error rises with observer error, while target improvement can hide nuisance-state movement. Under forward-only access, sparse aggregate measurements recover a finite-effect map with fewer interventions than coordinate patching. With gradient access, finite probes improve a local attribution map. Lifted measurements and Hessian-vector products recover interactions missed by first-order maps, while Tracr shows that the required family depends on the basis. On GPT-2-small IOI, the Name Mover-Negative Name Mover interaction is the largest held-out predictive term among three tested cross-group pairs. On Qwen-2.5-7B, finite calibration makes an additive refusal-response map adequate, so held-out error does not support pairwise lifting.

</details>


### [74] [Uncovering the Limits of Proof Sharing for Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19351)
*Kanak Das,Shubham Ugare,Bor-Yuh Evan Chang,Sasa Misailovic,Gagandeep Singh,Manu Sridharan*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Systematic study of template-based acceleration in robustness verification of neural networks; introduces jointly stable neurons and FastCert for automatic template distribution; reports 1.13x average speedup on L0 verification tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the uncertain effectiveness and limits of template-based reuse across diverse architectures, datasets, training methods, and properties in neural-network robustness verification; seek to understand when acceleration pays off.

Method: Empirical study; propose a metric of jointly stable neurons to explain variation; introduce FastCert to automatically distribute templates across layers and to drop templates unlikely to yield speedups; evaluate on covering-design based L0-verification tasks.

Result: Template subsumption rates vary widely across scenarios; the jointly stable neurons metric explains when speedups are unlikely; FastCert achieves an average 1.13x speedup over a baseline template-based reuse technique on a broad set of L0-verification tasks.

Conclusion: Template-based acceleration has variable impact depending on network and task; automated distribution (FastCert) can improve performance, but some scenarios yield little to no benefit; the work contributes both analysis (jointly stable neurons) and a practical method (FastCert) for robustness verification.

Abstract: Robustness verification of neural networks is increasingly important, due to their use in many critical domains. In certain scenarios, proof sharing has been shown to accelerate incomplete verification techniques by reusing intermediate-layer abstract states, or templates, across queries. However, questions remain as to the robustness of template-based acceleration across varying network architectures, properties, datasets, and training methods. In this work, we perform a systematic study of the effectiveness of template-based acceleration and its limits. Our study shows that template subsumption rates can vary widely across scenarios. We present a novel metric of jointly stable neurons to explain this variation, showing that in some cases template-based techniques are very unlikely to provide any speedup. Then, we present FastCert, a novel technique for automatically distributing templates across neural network layers to increase performance impact, eschewing templates entirely if they are unlikely to produce a speedup. Across a large set of covering-design based $L_0$-verification tasks, FastCert achieved an average speedup of 1.13x over an extant template-based reuse technique.

</details>


### [75] [Longitudinal Bayesian Learning of Continuous Disease Position across the Alzheimer's Disease Continuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19436)
*Yingying Zhang,Kun Zhao,Guodong Liu,Qi Huang,Pengfei Gu,Dongchul Kim,Erik Enriquez,Alex D. Leow,Paul M. Thompson,Heng Huang,Hongchang Gao,Liang Zhan,Haoteng Tang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Introduces Disease Continuum Positioning (DCP), a longitudinal Bayesian framework that estimates continuous Alzheimer's disease severity from longitudinal DTI, yielding a Disease Continuum Score (DCS) with uncertainty.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Alzheimer's disease progresses along a continuum, but most neuroimaging AI methods provide discrete labels or scores from cross-sectional data. A continuous, longitudinal assessment is needed to capture progression and predict conversion.

Method: Jointly models disease severity as a low-dimensional probabilistic latent variable using longitudinal DTI data with weak clinical supervision in a Bayesian framework; derives a Disease Continuum Score (DCS) and its uncertainty to position individuals along the AD continuum.

Result: On the ADNI cohort, DCP outperformed representative disease progression methods; DCS accurately reflects disease severity, shows strong clinical relevance, preserves longitudinal evolution, and predicts future conversion.

Conclusion: DCS offers a quantitative imaging-derived representation for continuous assessment of Alzheimer's disease progression beyond traditional diagnostic labels and clinical scores.

Abstract: Alzheimer's disease (AD) progresses as a continuous biological process, whereas most existing neuroimaging-based artificial intelligence methods remain limited to discrete diagnosis or clinical score prediction from cross-sectional imaging. In this work, we propose Disease Continuum Positioning (DCP), a longitudinal Bayesian Learning framework that continuously estimates disease severity from longitudinal diffusion tensor imaging (DTI). Specifically, DCP models disease severity as a low-dimensional probabilistic latent variable by jointly integrating longitudinal observations with weak clinical supervision, from which the proposed Disease Continuum Score (DCS) is derived to quantify an individual's position along the Alzheimer's disease continuum together with its associated uncertainty. Extensive experiments on the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative (ADNI) cohort demonstrate that DCP consistently outperforms representative disease progression methods. More importantly, comprehensive validation analyses show that DCS accurately characterizes disease severity, exhibits strong clinical relevance, preserves longitudinal disease evolution, and predicts future disease conversion. These results suggest that DCS provides a quantitative imaging-derived representation for continuous assessment of Alzheimer's disease progression beyond conventional diagnostic labels and clinical scores.

</details>
